A grown-up-sized long-sleeved double-zip printed one-piece zipper leisure suit in corduroy, with flared bell-bottom legs, covered edge to edge in a high-detail print that runs onto the sleeves and around the back, designed to look like the outfit of a Korean palace guard. The whole garment is the outfit, front, back, sleeves and bottom printed as the matching parts of it. The top shows a bright coloured robe with a sash, and the legs show wide trousers. An attached hood at the neck is printed as a black horsehair hat. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. Every part of the garment, from the collar band to the leg openings, is printed in the outfit's own cloth. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. It is a one-piece zipper leisure suit, a single garment with a full-length front zipper, a collar, pockets, a waist and legs, made in one piece of printed cloth. The garment is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the garment, in its own cloth and colours. The suit is the whole Korean palace guard outfit, not just a top, with a bright coloured robe with a sash, wide trousers on the legs, a black horsehair hat (as the hood), boots (as the feet), a flag sewn or attached to the suit. It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Novelty embellishments, beaded fringe along the hem and a quilted belt-like waist band with a big buckle shape. It reads as a joyful, exaggerated novelty onesie, stuffed with texture and detail.

The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire piece as one continuous print, wrapping around the sides to the back. The top shows a patterned tunic. An attached hood at the neck is printed as a headband. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the garment is outfit cloth. An attached hood at the neck is printed as a yellow crested hat. Turn it round and the back carries the rear of the outfit, with nothing left plain. The outfit's cloth covers the whole piece in one continuous print, collar, shoulders, sleeves, body and leg openings all in the outfit's own colours and textures. The print is cut exactly to the garment, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. A one-piece leisure jumpsuit with a zip front from collar to waist, the whole garment printed as the outfit. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole garment like a sewing pattern. The suit is the whole Tibetan monk outfit, not just a top, with a maroon robe with a gold sash, a yellow crested hat (as the hood), boots (as the feet), prayer beads sewn or attached to the suit. It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Novelty embellishments, gold-tone and silver-tone zipper pulls shaped like charms and ruffled fabric frills along the seams. The embellishments are sewn on as real raised fabric, so the suit has depth, texture and sparkle from every angle.
